### 2026-11-17T00:56:32Z [**rustviz/rustviz**](https://github.com/rustviz/rustviz)

```
# RustViz 2.1  RustViz draws ownership and borrowing timelines for Rust programs, for students learning Rust and the people teaching them. RustViz 2 plugs into `rustc` and reads the program's real HIR/MIR, so the diagram follows the borrow checker's view of your code (RustViz 1, which drew from hand-written annotations, stays at tag `rv1-final`).  RustViz 2.1 is the first release since 2.0.0 (May 2026) and collects about 470 merged PRs. It's about making the diagram trustworthy for teaching: many more Rust constructs are drawn, a long list of tooltips that said something false about Rust now say the right thing, borrows end where rustc ends them, and programs that used to crash the plugin now render.  ## Installing and upgrading  RustViz links `rustc_private`, so it needs one pinned nightly, `nightly-2026-05-01` (`rustc_plugin` 0.15.2); 2.0 used `nightly-2025-08-20` (#170). The CLI is now the short-named `rustviz` crate (#810), and `rustviz init` installs the toolchain and plugin:  sh cargo install rustviz        # the `rustviz` CLI (`cargo install rustviz-cli` still works) rustviz init                 # installs nightly-2026-05-01 + the plugin rustviz html foo.rs          # one self-contained HTML page; `rustviz svg foo.rs` for the two SVGs   mdBook users then add the preprocessor with `cargo install mdbook-rustviz` and tag code blocks ` rv `. The preprocessor runs the plugin directly on the host by default; set `RV_RUNNER=docker` to run it in the `rustviz-runner` container instead, which is safer for code you don't trust. As a library, `rustviz = "2"` re-exports `rustviz-lib`. From a checkout, `cargo xtask setup` does everything on Linux, macOS and Windows (#124). Plugin output is JSON, not `code:::timeline` text, so tools that parsed the old format need updating (#197).  ## The playground  Try it with no install at **<https://rustviz.github.io/>**. - A titlebar plus three resizable panels, which also give a clean presentation view; works on small screens (#81, #105). - The example dropdown follows the [RustViz tutorial](https://rustviz.github.io/tutorial/)'s chapters, from ownership and borrowing through conditionals, loops, closures, structs and threads (#811, #849). Examples are prerendered, so they appear instantly (#118). - Your own examples: editing an example forks it, and you can import / export them as `.rs` files; saves survive browser storage limits (#103, #104, #187). - Five colour themes matching mdBook's; it starts in Navy (dark) and remembers your choice (#1056). - Every figure in an mdBook book built with `mdbook-rustviz` has an "Open in Playground" link (#858). - The compile API runs on Google Cloud Run in a per-request sandbox (#163).  ## What RustViz now draws that it couldn't before  **Patterns and conditions** - `if let` / `while let` chains: bindings and their uses (#288), a clearer split-line layout (#582); a part that moves, lends, writes or assigns before a later part fails is drawn on the `else` branch too, instead of being refused (#581, #595, #608, #618, #716, #1075, #1086); a `while` condition that moves before it turns false leaves the value (maybe) moved after the loop (#985). - Match guards, including `if let` guards (#195); a guard that moves, assigns or lends an outer variable and then fails starts every later arm with that, as rustc sees it (#1051). - Or-patterns (`Ok(s) | Err(s) =>`): the arm binds its names (#536); a name bound from different places is "maybe moved" (#566, #617, #632); references, tuples and structs too, in `match`, `if let`, `while let` and `let` (#606, #951). - Composite patterns in `let`, `for` and `if let` (#110, #115, #399); `for (n, s) in pairs` moves or borrows its head (#596); `&` patterns (`for &w in &words`, `let (c, d) = *r;`) copy out of what the reference points to (#881, #887, #889, #895). - `ref` / `ref mut` bindings, `let ref r = s;` and `fn f(ref s: String)` are borrows, not copies (#387, #436, #435, #713). - Names in function-parameter patterns (#378) and nested struct fields (`r.tl.x`) (#359). - Diverging arms (`_ => unreachable!()`) and `else if` chains where every branch returns or diverges (#315, #493, #521, #368); an `if let` whose pattern always matches draws no failed-match path (#989, #999, #1008).  **Loops** - The bodies of `for`, `while` and `loop` are all drawn (#98); loop variables and loop-body locals get a start and an end (#273). - `let r = loop { ... break k; }`: `r` gets the value `break` carries (#460, #588); a reference out of a loop borrows what it borrowed, and a borrow made by one of several `break`s says which (#656, #671, #675, #714). - `for w in words` over an owned `Vec<String>` moves the collection into the loop's iterator and one element into `w` per iteration (#852); Copy elements are copies (#777). - A `for` loop's iterator keeps its collection borrowed until the loop ends, for `&v`, `v.iter()`, `v.iter_mut()`, `&mut` parameters and adapter chains (#748, #763, #876, #883, #891, #909, #1062, #1070, #1077, #1085, #1092); the iterator is dropped where the loop is left, including `break`, `continue 'outer`, `?` and `panic!()` (#529, #596, #615, #744). - Moves and assignments that come back round: a move in the body makes its start "maybe moved" on the next time round; a `let` first assigned in a loop drops the earlier iteration's value; a `return` inside a loop states its drops there (#788, #996, #1005, #1017, #1023, #1030, #1033, #1035, #1042, #1045, #1058, #1074).  **Temporaries** - A temporary borrowed by a `let` gets a column to the end of the block (#447); a call's result that nothing takes is a `(temporary)` and its drop is stated (#467). - Temporaries of macros, literals, `if let` / `while let` scrutinees, `for` heads, tuple scrutinees `(x, make())`, and struct / tuple initializers; a variable or field moved into one; what is left in a temporary after a field is moved out (#445, #571, #572, #573, #610, #616, #622, #643, #945, #957, #983, #988).  **Shadowing and scopes** - Each shadowing binding gets its own column (`s`, `s₂`) (#308); an `else if let` that reuses a name shares its column (#559); same-named variables of different types, or one that needs drop and one that doesn't, get separate columns (#619, #647, #924). - A `let` inside a branch, arm or let-chain block exists only there (#446, #534, #935, #986); sibling blocks each end their column (#607, #946).  **Box, Rc, statics, collections** - `Box`: a field moved out is drawn and the scope end says what is freed (#614, #620); taking apart a Box's tuple or struct payload (`let (s, n) = *b;`, `match *b`) moves only the parts that move, `*b = ..` refills them, and `**rb = v` drops only the old `String` (#896, #902, #911, #923, #944, #962, #963). - `Rc` / `Arc` / `Weak` drops say they lower a count rather than freeing the value (#464, #522). - A `static` gets a column in the function that uses it and is never dropped; reading a Copy `const` names the constant (#1054, #1064, #1072). - Array literals, `vec![..]` elements and copies out of an index (#353); tuples get a column per element (#341); types from `std` and other crates render as opaque owners (#92); unions as single-owner columns (#141).  **Fields, elements and references** - Writes to a field through a reference (`self.n += 1`), to an element (`v[i] = x`), through a reference stored in an element, and through `get_mut` / `&mut v[i]` / `iter_mut`, drawn on the struct's or collection's column (#148, #153, #440, #859, #862, #964, #1050), plus the matching reads and `&mut r.a` borrows (#357, #358, #455, #1091, #1099). - Assignments to a place with no column of its own, such as `t.0 = ...`, state the old value's drop (#468). - Deferred initialization `let x; x = ...` (#246); destructuring assignment `(a, b) = (b, a)` (#157); struct update `..base`, field-init shorthand and whole-struct moves (#208, #610).  **Closures, functions, operators, macros** - Closures: captures come from rustc's capture analysis (#95); by-value captures are moved in (#301), a `move` closure copies Copy captures (#375), by-reference captures are drawn for the call (#340), and a closure captures only the field or tuple element it uses (#402, #641, #1102). Calling an FnOnce closure moves it (#499). - Closures as values: `(w.f)()`, `adder(t)(1)`, `let h = g;` (#728); a returned closure takes its by-value captures along (#765); a boxed closure keeps its borrows until the box is dropped (#1106, #1108); a closure passed to a call that doesn't keep it borrows nothing afterwards (#1105). - A function bound to a local, and calls through it (#305). - Operators: arithmetic operands are copies (#263, #306), `acc += x` is not `a = b` (#310), comparisons borrow their operands (#386, #528); `self` methods and `String +` show the receiver as moved (#188); `s.len()` says the receiver is implicitly borrowed (#364). - Standard macros: `println!`, `format!`, `assert_eq!`, `panic!`, `dbg!`, `vec!` show the borrows and moves of their arguments (#99, #114, #206), and their variables are coloured in the code panel (#823, #826). - Returns: each branch of a returned `if` / `match`, and a returned call result, goes to the caller; `return *r` with `r: &i32` returns a copy (#350, #678, #680, #794, #808). Method chains (#154); `?` draws its early return (#454, #648, #667, #746).  ## Things it used to get wrong, now right  **Drops at early exits** (the ones students were most misled by) - A `return`, panic, `break`, `continue` or `?` drops what it leaves behind, there, including what its own branch declared (#411, #432, #451, #454, #521, #563, #646, #648); a let-else's diverging `else` states its drops (#923); a body that always returns ends the function on that row, with nothing drawn after it (#1016, #1025). - A reassignment drops the old value (#365), but only if it is still there (#403, #863); `*r = x` and `drop(x)` state the drop (#462); a collection's or enum's drop says what it holds, e.g. "including the String it holds, if any" for a `Result` (#545, #546, #701). - Copy values, references and `ManuallyDrop` don't claim a drop (#285, #322, #523); a Copy value's scope end just reads "x goes out of scope" (#799).  **Borrows end where the borrow checker ends them** - Borrow ends now come from rustc's own borrow-checker (NLL) dataflow (#669, #676), with one known gap (#855). - A moved `&mut` ends no borrow; a coerced `&mut` is a reborrow (#294, #937, #965); `(&mut s).push_str(..)` is a fresh borrow (#406); a `&mut` passed to a generic `T` is moved (#286). - References are matched to their own loan (#293): swapped references keep both lenders borrowed (#345), and a reference chosen by `if` / `match`, or a `let r;` given a loan in each arm, keeps every arm's lender borrowed (#398, #1083). - A borrow made inside one arm ends only on that arm; an old loan of a reassigned reference ends on the path that reaches the write (#672, #779, #797, #802, #804, #809, #948); a dot that ends two loans names each lender (#805). - Borrows through a reference, reborrows of elements and a borrow ended in a match arm (#401, #434, #397, #427, #498); a reference's borrow ends after the rest of its line uses it (#590, #601, #621). - A write drawn inside a borrow that is already over on its path says why rustc accepts it (#959). - `&x` or `&mut x` where `x` is itself a reference borrows the variable `x` (a value), not what it points to (#933, #943, #966, #975); a reference returned through a reference parameter (`&self.s`, `&mut v[0]`) is a reborrow and its borrow passes to the caller (#774, #785, #798, #1079, #1087).  **Values that hold borrows** (`Vec<&T>`, iterators, structs with reference fields) - `v.iter()`, `v.get(2)`, `vec![&s]`, `collect()`, `push(&s)` are drawn as borrowing (#394, #404, #433, #325, #991); a call's returned reference borrows what its signature ties it to (#339, #717). - The borrow moves with its holder and is held by every copy or clone of it (#650, #879, #998, #1009, #1018, #1022, #1103); borrows stored in structs last until the struct's last use, and overwriting a reference field or the whole struct is handled as rustc does (#537, #540, #568, #639, #654, #665, #1098). - A reference bound by a pattern borrows what rustc's lifetimes put in it (`Some(x)` on `pick(&a, &b)`) (#421, #720, #726, #764).  **"Resource" vs "value" wording** - "Resource" is said only for types that need drop; other non-Copy things are "values" (#485). This now covers Copy values (#321, #383, #523), borrows of values that need no drop (#550, #552, #869), closures holding only references (#795), and holders that do need drop (#1109). References never own their referent; moved and copied references say so (#167, #272, #320). - "initialized with a copy of x" replaces "initialized by copy from x" (#500); `&mut` borrows say the lender "can't be used" meanwhile, not "frozen" (#128); "until it's assigned again" only for `mut` bindings (#842). - Constructors (`Some(x)`, `Point { .. }`) and built-in operators are never named as actors or borrowers (#782, #877, #884, #915, #958, #972); no more "copy of Anonymous resource" (#548).  **Partial moves, conditional moves and maybe-initialized variables** - A partially moved struct says which fields it drops (#255); destructuring moves each field, not the whole struct (#384); a field moved out on some paths only drops "only if no branch moved it" (#441). - An `if` without `else` that moves is a conditional move (#334); every merge point says what each variable holds after it (#116, #120), following drop flags (#300); a Copy variable bound in every branch is "bound to a value" (#356). - A `let x;` assigned in only some branches is "maybe initialized" afterwards, and a drop flag decides its drops (#787, #934, #1038, #1040); `v = if ..` and `let (a, b) = if ..` pair each branch's value with its branch (#1014, #1024).  ## Crashes fixed - `&{}` (#149), `?` (#155), `RefCell` deref-assign and nested constructors (#165), macro-expanded right-hand sides and field-projection shapes (#83, #93), unit structs and nested-field moves (#332), `let r = *rr[0];` on `Vec<&&i32>` (#465), `let r = &words[1];` on `Vec<&str>` (#901). - `const` / `static` / `static mut` reads and writes (#142), union writes and tuple-struct field reads (#141), deferred initialization (#246), a closure called out of a struct field `(w.f)()` (#728), `*r = "x"` through `r: &mut &str` (#910), the Book's `enum Message { Move { x: i32, y: i32 } }` with a struct-like variant (#912), a field of a call in a tuple scrutinee (#983). - `cargo rv-plugin` no longer panics on a binary crate (`src/main.rs`); it draws the crate root rustc compiles (#753). - Timeline SVGs are well-formed XML, so a figure embedded as a standalone SVG no longer fails to load when a tooltip quotes `&&` (#1061). Code-panel markup survives `[_` in user source (#162); derived impls draw no ghost timelines (#166). - Compile errors and plugin failures show distinct messages, "This program doesn't compile" vs "RustViz can't visualize this yet" (#168), and remaining internal panics give a readable reason (#524). User macros that take a variable are refused with a message rather than drawn wrongly (#456).  ## Appearance and themes - Colour themes matching mdBook's five (Light, Rust, Coal, Navy, Ayu): figures in a book follow the reader's theme, and the playground has a picker (#1056). - A column that is mutably lent out draws a faint dotted line until the borrow ends, so the lend's events sit on the column (#961). - Code-panel syntax highlighting (#67); hovering a branch underlines only that arm, each segment has its own tooltip, and a dashed arm segment or arm diagonal says on hover what it means (#385, #423, #629, #846). - Events on one line of a column share one dot, so every tooltip is reachable (#329); tooltips appear at the cursor, wrap, and stay inside the window (#471, #541, #840); arrows are drawn beneath dots (#473); long struct member labels fit (#478); columns past the 12-colour palette keep their colours (#302); figures end just below the last code line (#847); shorter caption, "Hover over the visualization for more information." (#1063). - Many layout fixes for branches, joins, returns and borrow brackets (#129, #270, #360, #374, #381, #442, #488, #490, #494, #507, #516, #558, #593, #594, #601, #640, #715, #721, #750, #790, #848).  ## mdBook, CLI and plugin - `mdbook-rustviz`: "Open in Playground" under every figure, configurable or disabled with `playground-url` in `book.toml` (#858); long code lines scroll in the code panel while the timeline stays visible (#818, #838). - `// rustviz: skip` leaves a `let` or `fn` out of the trace; `// rustviz: hide` removes an item (`fn`, `struct`, `enum`, `impl`, `use`, ...) from the code panel, and warns when it hides nothing (#69, #825). - `cargo install rustviz` installs the CLI (#810); `cargo rv-plugin --file src/shapes.rs` draws one source file of a crate, and a file the crate doesn't compile gets a clear error (#753). - Plugin output is deterministic across runs (#214).  **Under the hood:** the Rust Book corpus (127 Book-style programs) and every fixture now run as tests that assert correct tooltips, not current output (#196, #819, #827, #865); toolchain bump checklist and `scripts/bump-nightly.sh` (#185); a probe-diff script for zero-output-change refactors (#820, #828–#837); CI builds on Windows again, builds the runner image on arm64 and smoke-tests the docker backend (#185, #275, #864); plugin crashes are tracked as issues labelled `crash` (#236).  ## Known limitations RustViz is a research tool: it only visualizes programs that compile (code with a borrow error shows rustc's message), and it handles a subset of Rust. The main gaps: - Loops are drawn as one pass over the body; nothing shows that it repeats (#853). - Closures: captures are drawn, but what the closure body does when it runs isn't shown on the captured variables' timelines (#133, #278, #347). - Your own `macro_rules!` macros aren't traced (#137); raw pointers (#194) and `async` / `await` (#135) aren't modelled. - `Rc`, `Arc`, `RefCell`, `Mutex`, trait objects, and a `Box` whose payload isn't taken apart, render as one owner column; `RefCell`'s runtime borrows aren't shown (#685). - Vec and array elements have no column of their own: writes are drawn on the collection's column and a replaced element's drop is stated only in its tooltip (#689). Method chains draw only the first call on the receiver's column (#687). - An `if let` chain or match guard that assigns a reference from another reference or a call, or sits inside a loop, is still refused (#1068).  Full list: [open issues labelled `limitation`](https://github.com/rustviz/rustviz/issues?q=is%3Aissue+is%3Aopen+label%3Alimitation). Crashes on valid programs: [open issues labelled `crash`](https://github.com/rustviz/rustviz/issues?q=is%3Aissue+is%3Aopen+label%3Acrash). If RustViz gets a program wrong, please [open an issue](https://github.com/rustviz/rustviz/issues/new) with the snippet. 
```

## English  - Fix Dida365 account binding by including login credentials, and update list and tag retrieval endpoints - Fix incorrect group labels and HTTP 403 errors when saving to Dida365 - Improve Dida365 save error checks and request-header rule cleanup, and correct saved-page links - Transfer Obsidian content through the clipboard to avoid long-note truncation in URLs; report clipboard failures explicitly - Make clipboard write permission optional and request it when binding or using Obsidian; saving replaces the current clipboard contents - Rename the existing Confluence integration to Confluence 6  ## 中文  - 修复滴答清单绑定时未携带登录凭据的问题，更新清单和标签读取接口 - 修复滴答清单未分组标题异常，以及保存时返回 403 的问题 - 改进滴答清单保存错误检查和请求头规则清理，修正保存后打开的页面地址 - Obsidian 改用剪贴板传递正文，避免长文通过 URL 传递时被截断；复制失败时明确报错 - 剪贴板写入权限改为可选，仅在绑定或使用 Obsidian 时按需申请；保存会覆盖当前剪贴板内容 - 将现有 Confluence 平台显示名称调整为 Confluence 6
